## Audit Extract and Generate fast paths for recent additions (2026-10-14)
- Confirmed the current READY cache uses source registry `catalogued-sources-v27-quest-world-loot-grants`. Extract keeps raw extracted files keyed to the game build and reuses them when catalog/prepared-data revisions change; it rebuilds the derived World Loot descriptors and renewable pickup class profiles from the cached sources. Renewable ingredient Blueprint packages are registered in the materials source group.
- Generate opens the READY cache through `CachedAssetExtractor`, so these paths do not re-extract source assets from the installed game archives. Ingredients use Extract-prepared class profiles for pool planning, then stage/recheck only the participating pickup Blueprints from cache and patch their CDO rewards in a batched UAssetAPI call. Quest and Hot Spring grants are also read from cache and serialized in package batches.
- The fixed-width binary writer is only the fast path for supported World Loot map layouts. Ingredients and structured Quest/Hot Spring grants use their validated structural serializers instead; unsupported map layouts also fall back to structural patching. This is an accurate scope boundary, not a missing cache integration.
- Updated the start of Rules to explain that originals stay untouched and how to uninstall. Validation: the targeted UI integration test passed. No release was created.

## Fix generated material maps crashing during save load (2026-10-11)
- The reported crash was in generated map `/Game/Map/MainWorld/MainWorld/_Generated_/9SA0L0VMEDTNFAPWAUUOPE54T`, not the shop table. The generation report confirmed this map was changed only by the renewable Ingredients feature.
- When UAssetAPI added missing material Blueprint imports, it changed export-table serial sizes/offsets but Generate restored the original opaque `.uexp`. That mismatch explains the game's `Serial size mismatch` crash. Material staging now preserves each source export size and shifts its offset by the new `.umap` header delta, then reopens the package and validates every export against the unchanged sidecar.
- Reproduced the import patch on the exact cached map: UAssetAPI changed export 170 from 264 bytes to 262 while the source `.uexp` stayed unchanged. The patch restored that 2-byte difference, rebased all 122 export rows, and reopened the package successfully.
- The Pre-order weapon experiment has been removed from the UI and Generate path for now. Existing saved settings safely ignore its obsolete key; shop stock fields remain untouched.
- Validation: 114 focused core, material, shop, pipeline, and UI tests passed; UAssetAPI Release build completed with 0 warnings/errors; Python `compileall` passed; the exact cached map reproduction reopened and validated successfully.

## Fix Extract validation for Healing pickup Blueprints (2026-10-11)
- Extract failed after cache promotion because cache inspection incorrectly required Golden Seed/Blood Bead physical pickup classes to have renewable ingredient hierarchy profiles. Those healing pickups use separate prepared templates and are not members of the renewable ingredient class pool.
- Cache inspection now requires reset/hierarchy evidence only for `RENEWABLE_INGREDIENT`; healing pickup descriptors retain their own structural and prepared-template checks. Renewable profile failures include the class and map/export identity for diagnosis.
- Added a regression test reproducing an installed Golden Seed Blueprint without a renewable profile. Validation: cache and generation pipeline suites passed (58 tests); Python `compileall` passed. The failed promotion restored the prior cache, and no game files, `~mods`, or release artifacts were changed.

## Add a separate renewable ingredient pool (2026-10-11)
- Enabled the `Ingredients` option for respawning world pickups. It shuffles renewable resource classes only among compatible renewable locations, independently from World Loot; the corresponding entries remain protected in the World Loot catalog.
- The current catalog contains 2,253 map/export rows: 1,278 canonical pickups and 975 mirrored copies in alternate maps. Mirrors reuse the canonical assignment and do not add extra source items or spoiler entries. Per-map and Blueprint-parent pools preserve the source-class multiset; assignments are limited to classes already imported by each destination and its mirror maps. If a safe permutation is unavailable, the pickup stays vanilla.
- Added all renewable resource class packages to Extract's prepared dependency registry and bumped the prepared-data revision. Existing raw extracted data can be reused; users need one `Update Prepared Data` before generating with the new option, not a full re-extract.
- Validation: Generate and UI integration suites passed (68 tests); focused renewable catalog/planner/dependency checks passed; Python `compileall` passed. A broader foundation run also surfaced an unrelated existing catalog expectation mismatch (`BASE` expected 2,389, current data has 2,390). Generated-package gameplay validation remains pending. No game files, `~mods`, or release artifacts were changed.

## Compact spoiler placement, diagnostics shortcuts, and low-memory Generate (2026-10-10)
- Installed spoiler output is now named exactly `Spoiler.txt` and is owned beside the mod trio in `~mods`. The header has seed, local creation date, aggregate randomized-slot/unchanged counts, and app version; feature sections and changed replacements remain below it. Successful cleanup no longer copies a second spoiler to the output root and removes a prior app-format copy. A generated legacy `CV2Randomizer_Spoiler.txt` is migrated only when its previous ownership hash matches; edited legacy files are preserved and migration stops safely. Empty installs leave no spoiler copy in app output.
- Removed the Logs row from Diagnostic and added Open Settings Folder and Open Cache Folder beside Open Output Folder and Clear Extracted Game Data.
- Audited Generate memory behavior: the large World Loot serializer is the only Generate stage with a memory-based worker scheduler. It already uses one worker when RAM cannot support parallel work; after the scheduler change, one worker always proceeds and RAM only controls extra parallelism. Other patch helpers and Retoc packaging execute as subprocess stages without a preflight RAM rejection. Genuine Windows commit exhaustion or a helper's own failure can still stop a run; successful staging/installation remains validated and transactional.
- Validation: focused spoiler/core/installer/UI/memory/World Loot and no-op/cache Generate suites passed (`97 passed, 4 subtests`); `compileall` passed. The broader Generate-pipeline suite has 10 existing fixture/catalog failures (9 expect the unique Stinger world location without the audited Shop alternate; 1 expects an older Bequeathed Forma catalog), with 24 tests passing. These mismatches are unrelated to the spoiler, UI, or memory changes.

## Deterministic randomizer inventory export (2026-10-07)
- Added a Diagnostic export that writes one row per known reward/location to CSV and JSON, plus unique-Article and category/system-owner summaries. It reads the production catalogs, reservations, and World Loot planner eligibility; it does not change generation or patching behavior.
- The report records current options separately from intrinsic eligibility, marks renewable ingredients disabled, preserves protected/unsupported/unavailable records, and leaves installed-game availability blank when no validated extracted cache was supplied. The summary distinguishes per-category counts from the aggregate `WORLD_LOOT` system-owner row.
- Added deterministic output and known-case validation tests, and wired the export into the background UI worker. The export opens its output directory after completion.

## Recover partial edits to the installed Randomizer containers (2026-10-06)
- Diagnosed Generate & Install failing because the installed `CV2Randomizer_P.pak` still matched the app's ownership record, while `.ucas` and `.utoc` did not. The generation and Retoc packaging had completed; the installer stopped before replacing any files.
- Added a controlled recovery path for a registered Randomizer package when at least one unchanged container confirms its identity: copy the existing registered files and prior ownership record into `installation/recovery_backups/`, verify their hashes, then perform the existing atomic replacement. If the whole trio differs or a destination is not registered to the app, installation still refuses and leaves files untouched.
- Surface the recovery backup path in the application log and add tests for anchored recovery, backup integrity, unrelated-mod preservation, and refusal when no unchanged container remains.

## Clear extracted game data without freezing the UI (2026-10-06)
- Moved the Diagnostic tab's cache deletion onto the existing background task worker. The bottom Status panel now shows “Clearing extracted game data…” with an indeterminate busy bar until deletion finishes, then reports completion.
- Generation controls stay disabled during deletion, and Clear is disabled while cache inspection is active to avoid deleting files another worker is reading. If deletion fails partway, the app rechecks the cache before allowing generation again.
- Added a UI integration test that blocks the deletion worker and verifies the UI remains responsive, the busy indicator is active, and the cache state updates after completion.

The user says the same save opens after removing both `~mods` and `LogicMods`; both were changed together, so that test does not isolate our package from any other mod.

- A concrete Material serialization gap was found: `MaterialPatcher` called `UAsset.Write` and moved its reserialized `.uexp` into output even though V1 changes only export-map `ClassIndex` fields. In the failed full output, 21 of 73 material-patched map sidecars differed from their exact pre-Materials input. The helper now restores the input `.uexp` bytes after writing the header, and the Python serializer refuses any sidecar byte change.
- The changed helper passed a real map integration: the requested Blueprint class reopened correctly and the input/output `.uexp` SHA-256 matched. Added two Python regressions for accepting preserved sidecars and rejecting/cleaning rewritten sidecars. Full suite: 101 tests pass; Python compileall and the .NET helper build pass.
- Built ignored, non-installed diagnostic packages under `workspace/phase17-validation/isolated-packages/`: `CV2DiagWorldLoot_P` (571 map assets), `CV2DiagMaterialHeaders_P` (73 map assets), `CV2DiagMapHeadersOnly_P` (630 World Loot + Materials map assets with original sidecars), and `CV2DiagShops_P` (2 DataTables). All pass `retoc verify`; none were installed.

## Phase 17 Materials isolation result and serial-size fix (2026-09-25)
- The user reports `world-loot-only` loads, `shops-only` loads, and `world-material-headers-only` crashes at `/Game/Map/MainWorld/MainWorld/_Generated_/9SA0L0VMEDTNFAPWAUUOPE54T` with Expected read size 22 / Actual read size 24. This isolates the failing change to the Materials map edits.
- Root cause confirmed on that exact map: the previous material helper reserialized the `.umap` header and calculated export serial sizes from its reserialized `.uexp` (69,697 bytes), then restored the original `.uexp` (69,699 bytes). The header grew from 45,732 to 47,103 bytes, leaving a two-byte serial-size discrepancy that matches the in-game error.
- The helper now copies the original package and edits only existing four-byte export `ClassIndex` fields. It refuses target classes not already imported by the map. The planner now shuffles only within one map and immediate Blueprint parent, so every assigned class is already present in that map and class families stay bounded.
- Real integration on the failing map changed export 46 from `BP_it23_001_A_13_C` to existing import `BP_it23_001_A_16_C`: `.umap` stayed 45,732 bytes with one differing byte, `.uexp` stayed byte-identical at 69,699 bytes, and UAssetAPI reopened the requested class. The old serial size mismatch is eliminated in the binary output; runtime confirmation remains with the user.
- Rebuilt ignored diagnostic `workspace/phase17-validation/isolated-packages/materials-only-raw-patch/packaged/CV2DiagMaterialsRaw_P.{pak,ucas,utoc}` with 495 assignments and 239 locked locations. `retoc verify` passed, copied hashes match, and `installed=false`.
- Added map-local and Blueprint-parent grouping tests. Full Python suite passes 103 tests; `compileall` and .NET build pass with no warnings or errors. No files in the game installation were changed.

## Extract / Cache / Randomize workflow (2026-09-29)
- Added `GameDataCacheStore` with explicit `NOT_EXTRACTED`, `READY`, `OUTDATED`, `INVALID`, and `EXTRACTING` states. The persistent immutable cache is under the app-owned `platformdirs.user_cache_dir(APP_NAME) / "extracted"` directory, with one entry per game version, archive fingerprint, and catalog revision.
- Cache metadata (`cache_info.json`) binds each entry to the canonical installation path, executable SHA-256, IoStore archive fingerprint, game/catalog/cache revision, exact required source packages, central source registry, available and missing optional packages, invalid sources, package inventory, and cached file names/sizes/SHA-256 values. Startup checks run in `GameDetectionThread`; mismatched installation/build/revision/required-source definitions and missing/corrupt files cannot produce a ready cache.
- Added an explicit `Extract Game Data` / `Re-extract Game Data` flow. It reuses `_known_source_packages` and `IoStoreAssetExtractor`, extracts required core packages and every present supported catalog source, then prefetches recursive World Loot/Materials Blueprint dependencies through the existing resolvers. Rebuild happens in an isolated cache directory; promotion occurs only after staged validation, and the previous entry is retained if the candidate fails validation.
- Normal UI Generate now requires a ready cache entry and uses `CachedAssetExtractor` for package inventory/materialization. It copies only the selected systems' available package inputs into per-generation workspaces; existing patchers and Retoc package validation remain in place. The cache files are not patch outputs. Legacy direct source injection remains for internal fixtures/tools but is not submitted by normal UI Generate. Two real pipeline fixture generations with different seeds confirmed zero calls to the installed extractor and unchanged cache hashes.
- Before readiness, seed/randomizer/preset/Generate/Remove controls are disabled; game path, mappings, Extract, and Clear Cache stay available. Cache-backed feature availability enables only systems with relevant packages. Re-extraction is replacement-safe; Clear Cache deletes only the app-owned extracted-data directory and returns the UI to the locked state.
- Added `packaged/Spoiler.txt` to successful non-dry-run output, grouped by feature and containing only actual changed item identities in a short `A -> B` form. Self-maps, entries with identical visible labels, and empty sections are omitted; the file is removed on failures. The existing optional diagnostic `spoiler_log.csv` remains separate. Enemy Drop snapshots now carry short Article labels from the shared UAssetAPI helper so the player-facing spoiler never needs fingerprints.
- Updated `docs/design.md` and `docs/plan.md`. Added focused cache identity/integrity/immutability tests, cache-only multi-seed generation coverage, UI gating coverage, and Spoiler formatting/rollback tests.
- Verification: `.venv\\Scripts\\python.exe -m compileall -q src/cv2randomizer tools tests` passes; the full Python suite passes **260 tests with 9 optional integrations skipped**; `dotnet build tools/uassetapi-worldloot/UAssetAPIWorldLoot.csproj --no-restore` succeeds with 0 warnings and 0 errors. `data/versions/` has no officially supported executable signature, so the UI correctly keeps Extract disabled for an unrecognized installed build. No live extraction, game installation, package installation, or gameplay validation was performed.

## Game-path check status normalization (2026-09-30)
- Fixed both background game-detection and extracted-cache callbacks to compare normalized absolute paths. Previously a path entered with `/` separators (such as the Steam path) did not string-match the worker's Windows `\\` path, so both results were discarded and the UI stayed on “Checking extracted game data…”.
- Added a UI integration regression that feeds slash-variant paths through both callbacks and confirms their results are applied.
- Verification at that initial fix: `compileall` and all 11 `test_ui_integration.py` tests passed via `unittest`. That pass still gated extraction on an official signature; the following local-validation section supersedes that behavior without adding an official version definition.

## Local validation for unregistered game builds (2026-09-30)
- Changed the desktop workflow so a structurally valid install with no exact signature can be identified as `local-unverified-<executable SHA-256>`. Official support remains strictly tied to exact definitions in `data/versions/`; this local identity is not written there.
- The local cache binds to installation path, executable hash, archive fingerprint, and catalog revision. Because an unknown build has no version-reviewed core list, extraction considers the existing catalog registry opportunistically, skips missing packages, validates cached sidecars/hashes, and requires at least one catalogued source package before marking the cache ready.
- The desktop UI now enables Extract for this local identity, and enables local generation only after the extracted cache validates. Local generation requires that cache and its manifest records `version_validation: local-unverified`; the support panel continues to state that this build is not official. Parser/patch/package validation remains unchanged and fails closed.
- Read-only detection against the selected Steam installation returned `local-unverified` with the expected `CodeVein2-Win64-Shipping.exe`; no live asset extraction, mod generation, installation, or game-file modification was performed in this implementation pass.
- Verification: the full unittest suite passed 265 tests with 9 optional integrations skipped; compileall and focused versioning/cache/UI/pipeline tests passed. One additional cache metadata regression was added afterward and passed in a focused run.
- Follow-up extraction fix: local-unverified builds have an empty mandatory-core package set, so skip the required-package extraction call when it is empty and continue with the present catalogued optional packages. Added a pipeline regression that fails if the extractor receives an empty batch and confirms the local cache reaches `READY` from a present catalog package.
- Final verification after the fix: full unittest suite passes 267 tests with 9 optional integrations skipped; `compileall` passes. The failed app attempt only wrote to its isolated rebuild staging directory, which the pipeline removes on failure; no game assets or installed mod files were changed.

- Extraction now persists a compact `world_loot_dependency_index` (schema, game/catalog identity, map/export identities, and Blueprint parent package closure). Helper results retain compact paths/digests rather than export snapshots. Per-package diagnostics record assignment count, input size, load/patch/save/validation/total timings, managed heap and working-set measurements; a conditional collection runs only after large source graphs are released. UAssetAPI's `UAsset` is not disposable. A per-helper .NET GC heap limit of 2 GiB is passed in the child environment. A real four-assignment map that previously peaked at 24,171 MiB completed and reopened in 2.03 s with a 221 MiB observed process peak under this cap. In the completed full Generate, 1,558 World Loot assignments produced 1,511 changed rewards across 593 packages; 47 had unchanged reward tuples and produced no patch. The map helper wall time was 1,107.82 s (about 18m28s); the package stage wall time estimate was 1,110.48 s (about 18m30s), with 262.42 s spent inside UAssetAPI load/patch/save/reopen work. The largest package took 15.04 s; 95% of package peaks were at or below 389 MiB, mean peak was 185.1 MiB, 16 packages exceeded 1 GiB, and the maximum working set was 2,055 MiB. Package 356 (`/Game/Map/MainWorld/MainWorld/_Generated_/8WUYH2RH72EBKXUXYVSD3QG0J`) was the slowest/highest-peak case: 3.8 MiB input, 3.38 s load, 0.08 s patch, 0.16 s save, and 11.40 s reopen/validation. A different large export graph showed managed heap dropping from 1,027 MiB to 50 MiB after its source reference was released and the single large-phase collection ran. The helper is process-isolated per map, so its peak did not accumulate across packages.
- Renamed cache-backed Generate stages so they say “Loading … from cache”; added explicit package-level World Loot progress. Updated `docs/design.md` for dormant Materials, the static cache index, helper isolation/heap management, and the current UI layout.
- Benchmark seed `worldloot-bounded-memory-20260930` completed end-to-end in 1,417.38 s (23m37s): the persistent cache was read, World Loot and its direct grants were patched, and Retoc produced a verified `.pak` (347 B), `.ucas` (152,642,722 B), and `.utoc` (98,467 B) plus `Spoiler.txt`. `randomize_materials` was false; `install_after_generation` was false. No mod was copied to the game or installed. The local executable remains `local-unverified`; this is not official version support. Before the fix, our interrupted unbounded run reached 24,171 MiB helper peak in one map and was stopped after 40/592 packages; this completed run stayed below 2,055 MiB across all 593 map helpers.

## Extract memory and Enemy Drop helper stability pass (2026-09-30)
- Reproduced the original Extract pressure in `WorldLootDependencyResolver.stage_for_locations()` during `inspect-map-batch`. The Release UAssetAPI helper (`UAssetAPIWorldLoot.exe`, PID 32168 in the captured run) was one long-lived process loading an eight-map batch. Its working set reached **20,867,354,624 bytes (19.43 GiB)** and private committed bytes reached **44,276,346,880 bytes (41.24 GiB)**. Total system use reached **30,375,321,600 bytes (28.29 GiB)** on a **31.08 GiB** machine, leaving **2.80 GiB** available. Retoc and the Python parent were not the owner. Large UAsset parse graphs and the .NET heap/native allocator accumulated within a multi-map process lifetime; forcing GC every two map results did not contain its working set or commit. The process/batch lifetime, missing Extract budget, and absent .NET heap cap let a map-inspection helper pressure the whole system.
- Extract map inspection and Blueprint-parent resolution now process one package per short-lived Release helper and retain only compact JSON/index data in Python. The helpers receive `DOTNET_GCHeapHardLimit=0x80000000` (2 GiB); package peak working sets feed later job estimates. `MemoryBudgetScheduler` combines available and total RAM, active reservations, package size, recent job peak, and a reserve `max(2 GiB, 15% physical RAM)`. Its worker ceiling rounds nominal 8/16/32 GB PCs to at most 1/2/3 workers, and low availability can reduce concurrency to one or pause admission for up to 30 seconds. It reports a cache-preserving error if a job cannot safely start. The scheduler also gates Retoc inventory/extraction calls. A first safe rerun exposed a separate Windows file-sharing rule: concurrent UAssetAPI processes cannot open one shared `.usmap` path, so Extract now creates and hash-verifies one temporary mapping copy per helper lane before any helper starts.
- Extract transactionality remains intact. The first memory-safe attempt stopped on the `.usmap` sharing error, removed only its owned incomplete `.rebuild-*` tree, and left the prior READY cache available. After per-worker mapping copies, the final fresh Extract completed in **79.954 s** (helper diagnostic elapsed **78.162 s**) and the cache is **READY** with **4,732 files / 174,606,811 bytes**. On **31.09 GiB** physical RAM, peak system use was **9.45 GiB**, minimum available RAM **21.63 GiB**, Python peak working set **207.0 MiB**, non-Python child peak working set **313.1 MiB** (`retoc.exe`), and maximum scheduled heavy workers **3**. The largest sampled package helper job was `/Game/Map/MainWorld/MainWorld/_Generated_/7D8WV2PLG5RVP1IAK3A95TAO0`: **139.4 MiB** process peak and about **63.9 MiB** managed heap; the overall managed heap peak was **74.0 MiB**. The external process-tree observer's peak sum including the Python child was **549.3 MiB**. Detailed samples are saved under the app cache's `diagnostics/latest-extract.json` and the ignored `workspace/stability-pass/` probe output.
- The Enemy Drop request was reproduced outside the GUI with all three cached source tables present. Each had both source sidecars: BASE **21,183 / 36,381 bytes**, AFTER_ENDING **2,365 / 1,117**, and DLC **8,181 / 24,252**. Their parsed table sizes were 154/877 slots, 3/27, and 76/594; the catalog has 120/711, 3/27, and 66/510 eligible rows/slots. The original failing direct request contained **1,239 changed-slot assignments** (709 BASE, 26 AFTER_ENDING, 504 DLC). Its first assignment was BASE `Acid_Military[0]` <- DLC `Uni_P_HmmrG[2]`. The null was `PropertyTypeName.Nodes` on an Article property: this optional type-name node list was absent in a valid property, while the helper assumed it always existed. The source rows, sidecars, cache resolution, and serialized `tables`/`sourceTables` payload were valid; cache/UI/staging refactors were not the cause.
- `BindPropertyNames` now guards absent type-name nodes, preserves them when missing, recursively rebinds actual FNames, and adds table/row/source context while writing full .NET stack traces. Python validates canonical active source sets, BASE presence, destination/source slot identities against inspected snapshots, reward fingerprints, duplicate destinations, package roots, and `.uasset/.uexp` presence before patching. The C# helper independently validates null/malformed request entries, canonical source availability, changed destination subsets, paths, sidecars, rows, source fingerprints, and reopened tables. Failures save a sanitized, path-relative diagnostic request. Real .NET inspection passed BASE, BASE+AFTER_ENDING, BASE+DLC, and all three; an optional-only DLC destination patch sourced from BASE reopened with the source bundle and unchanged destination metadata. The exact original 1,239-assignment request was replayed directly through the helper with return code 0; all three outputs reopened. Cross-table mixing remains the existing automatic feature behavior.
- Full local Generate used seed `stability-world-enemy-drops-20260930` and completed in **212.222 s** with World Loot and Enemy Drops enabled. World Loot assigned 1,558 destinations and patched 593 packages. Enemy Drops assigned 1,248 slots across all three tables, including 642 cross-table assignments; all three tables were patched and reopened, with protected/unresolved rows and non-reward fields validated unchanged. Retoc verified 632 package paths and produced `CV2Randomizer_P.pak` (**347 B**), `.ucas` (**152,766,097 B**), `.utoc` (**99,039 B**), plus `Spoiler.txt` (**286,997 B**). Installation was disabled; no `~mods` or vanilla game file was modified. The executable remains `local-unverified` and was not added to `data/versions/`. Cross-table gameplay confirmation remains open.
- Added memory budget and helper contract regressions. Final verification: **288 tests passed, 9 optional integration tests skipped**; `compileall -q src tests tools` passed; `dotnet build ... -c Release --no-restore` passed with **0 warnings / 0 errors**. The Phase 21 fixed-offset World Loot fast path remains disabled and unchecked.

## Retoc uncompressed-container validation (2026-09-30)

- Identified the checked-in tool as the pinned upstream Windows x64 `retoc_cli 0.1.5` build. ## S2-B crash and read-only ArticleDataPath diagnosis (2026-10-01)
- User confirmed S2-B CRASH. The matrix now records S2-NAMEMAP PASS, S2-A PASS, and S2-B CRASH; the isolated runtime trigger is the second assignment into `Shop_Jadwiga_Item[0]`. No production source or generated container was changed during diagnosis.

The S2-B identity is from the global World Article pool; the bisection artifact did not retain the exact source location. The full source generation used Shop/World mixing and sent 347 world candidates to Shop destinations.
- Read-only binary comparison found the likely invalid identity spelling: world/catalog reward `DA_Item_MatLevelUpJailA_07` was normalized to the base FName plus Number 8, which resolves/render-formats as `_7` and loses the leading zero. Existing vanilla Shop rows store the literal `_07` package/object names with FName Numbers 0/0 (NameMap indices 111/484). S2-B instead writes base-name indices 626/739 with Numbers 8/8. S0/S2-NAMEMAP retain vanilla bytes; known-good S1 uses Numbers 0/0; S2-A passes with non-padded Numbers 2/2.
- The parser reads FName numbers but its catalog equality check compares only base package/object strings, so the invalid `_07`→`_7` transformation can reopen and pass validation. This is a strong root-cause candidate supported by runtime isolation and exact serialized comparisons; a corrected-encoding gameplay test has not been made.
- Detailed evidence, the 26 possible vanilla World catalog origins, generation settings, raw bytes, and corrected source attribution are in `workspace/runtime-regression/s2-b-root-cause-readonly.json` and `cases/current-s2-isolation-20260930/s2_isolation_report.json`. Await review before any production or asset change.

## S2-B exact FName diagnostic (2026-10-01)
- Built `S2-B-FIXED-FNAME` from the already-crashing S2-B case. It keeps the same 762-entry destination NameMap and changes only the target `ArticleDataPath` bytes in `DT_ShopLineup.uexp`; the `.uasset` is byte-identical to crashing S2-B. No production source, unrelated asset, Retoc configuration, or Oodle behavior changed.
- Read the extracted vanilla `DT_ShopLineup` from the app's extracted-asset cache. Two real references confirm the exact representation: `Shop_Jadwiga_Item[18]` and `Shop_PI_Normal1[5]` both use path literal `/Game/Blueprint/Article/Item/EnhancementMaterial/DA_Item_MatLevelUpJailA_07` at destination NameMap index 111 with Number 0, and object literal `DA_Item_MatLevelUpJailA_07` at index 484 with Number 0. Both resolve with the zero-padded `_07` intact.
- The fixed diagnostic reuses those destination-local entries (no source-package indices are copied): path index 111/Number 0 and object index 484/Number 0. Its 20-byte field is `6f00000000000000e40100000000000000000000`; this exactly matches the serialized bytes in both vanilla references. ## Final UI, seed, and successful-run cleanup pass (2026-10-01)
- The main window now creates a fresh 63-bit numeric seed before restoring settings. It no longer restores the previously saved seed; the user can edit the displayed seed, changing options leaves it alone, and Generate refuses a blank seed instead of silently choosing another one. The internal seed normalizer/planners remain deterministic for an explicit seed.
- `Spoiler.txt` now starts with `Seed: <resolved display seed>` followed by a blank line and the existing grouped spoiler sections. The value comes from the `NormalizedSeed` passed to the generation planners.
- Removed the user-facing Generate Only button, Recommended / Everything Safe / Disable All presets, and Install after generation checkbox. The desktop flow always requests Generate & Install. The reusable engine's internal non-install path remains available. Legacy `install_after_generation` settings are ignored on load and omitted from saved UI settings.
- Game Path now shows `C:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\steamapps\common\CodeVein2` as placeholder text; the field remains empty until an actual path is entered or selected.
- Successful Generate & Install now verifies each committed container's SHA-256 in `~mods`, atomically retains the latest `Spoiler.txt` at the app output root, and then removes only current/stale workspaces recognized as app-owned direct children of `output/generated/`. A marker identifies new workspaces; legacy folders are removed only when their generated name and standard child layout match. Cache, extracted/prepared indexes, resources, catalogs, tools, settings, persistent logs, the installed trio, and the retained spoiler are outside cleanup targets. Failed runs leave the READY cache untouched and preserve the failure workspace/log.
- A cleanup report records temporary bytes before/after, reclaimed bytes, removed generation directories, retained spoiler, and any cleanup warning. Activity shows cleanup progress and ends with exactly `Randomizer completed`; failure paths do not emit that message.
- A saved legacy `output_dir` is accepted only when its resolved canonical path is inside the app-owned data directory. Added a regression test proving an external directory and its contents remain untouched and are not selected as cleanup targets.
- Added tests for fresh seeds across two startups despite a saved seed, option changes not regenerating a seed, exact manual-seed use, placeholder/path distinction, removed controls/default options, seed-first spoiler, successful install hash verification and cleanup, stale workspace cleanup, READY-cache retention after cleanup/failure, and a second cache-only Generate that makes no installed-game extraction calls.
- Measured the real local extracted-cache directory read-only at 174,820,610 bytes (about 166.6 MiB). In the fake-game Generate & Install integration test, temporary generation data went from 653,141 bytes to 0 and 653,141 bytes were reclaimed. The READY-cache repeat test reclaimed 961,044 bytes on its first run (including a stale prior workspace) and 955,292 bytes on the second run; each ended at 0, retained its spoiler, and left source-cache hashes unchanged. These are test-fixture sizes, not a full-game workload estimate.
- Validation: focused core/spoiler/UI/pipeline suite: 61 passed; `compileall src tests`: passed. The complete suite initially produced 296 passed, 9 skipped, and one unrelated World Loot dependency test failure. The failure repeated when run alone: its adaptive memory scheduler saw about 4.3 GiB available while the fixture was estimated to need 2,560 MiB plus a 4,774 MiB reserve. After the final changes, all remaining tests passed when that single test was deselected: 298 passed, 9 skipped, 1 deselected, 16 subtests passed. No World Loot code/test was changed in this pass. No real game installation or user `~mods` directory was modified.

No Blood Code package was overwritten/reverted. The old 37/37 counter was an intermediate-planning claim and did not itself prove final container contents; the new pre-Retoc invariant now reopens final staged packages and fails generation on any missing package or wrong reward.
- The regression audit’s same-seed full Generate used seed `2145852879268247759` from the persistent READY cache after cleanup. Final counts: World Loot 1,555; Shops 337 plus 94 NPC-trader assignments; Enemy Drops 1,248 planned / 1,235 changed; Blood Codes 37 / 37 changed; Boosters 102 planned / 101 changed; Bequeathed Formae 2 / 2 changed; Extractor 43; Initial Inventory 3. ## QtWidgets DLL load failure in release 0.1 (2026-10-01)
- Reproduced the reported frozen-app error. PyInstaller had copied `icuuc.dll` and `icudt78.dll` from an unrelated Poppler directory on the developer `PATH`. The bundled ICU exports version-suffixed names such as `ucnv_open_78`, while this Qt build imports unsuffixed ICU entry points; loading that DLL fails with Windows error 127 (`The specified procedure could not be found`). With those transitive ICU DLLs removed, Qt resolves Windows' ICU implementation and imports normally.
- Updated `scripts/build_release.ps1` to remove and reject transitively collected ICU DLLs from the PyInstaller bundle. Added a `--startup-smoke-test` entry point that constructs `QApplication` without displaying the main window; the builder now requires a successful frozen-process exit before zipping.
- Focused startup/resource tests passed: 12 passed, 3 subtests. The candidate ZIP contained `CV2.usmap`, `oo2core_9_win64.dll`, Retoc, and the self-contained UAssetAPI helper; it contained no ICU DLLs. The packaged Qt startup smoke exited 0 with the full developer `PATH` still present.
- Full Python suite: 303 passed, 9 skipped, 16 subtests passed; one World Loot dependency test failed because the host reported 1.36–1.37 GiB free while the test job requires 7,334 MiB. Re-running that test alone produced the same resource-budget failure, unrelated to the Qt/release changes.
- Promoted the candidate to `release/0.1/`. The prior release folder is preserved at `workspace/release-backup/0.1-before-qtwidgets-fix-20261001/`. ZIP size: 94,440,105 bytes; SHA-256: `10da1707957d6b9f4da627b9ccbf8308de5240f03637ceced23f658bd17214e2`. Promoted EXE startup smoke exits 0; no game installation files or `~mods` files were changed.

## Healing clone package identity collision fixed (2026-10-03)
- Resumed after an interrupted local validation and corrected the Healing Upgrade method described in the preceding entry. The old map-instance `ArticleBulkInfo` override did not make the rewards observable in gameplay. Each of the 38 physical Golden Seed/Blood Bead locations now uses a unique source Blueprint/CDO clone; the map actor's `ClassIndex` and `TemplateIndex` point to its clone, and only the clone CDO's serialized `ArticleDataPath` changes.
- Root cause of the first clone package output: although clone files had unique physical virtual paths, their serialized UE5 `FolderName` still named the source Blueprint. Retoc therefore assigned duplicate IoStore package IDs and reduced 38 physical clones to two package identities. The helper now sets `FolderName` to the clone's unique virtual path and validates that identity after reopening. A same-source two-clone packaging diagnostic now lists two distinct packages and passes Retoc `verify`.
- The clone payload keeps Count and `bNotification`; map `.uexp` stays unchanged, and map `.uasset` changes only as needed for clone imports and class/template redirects. Source Blueprint/CDO files remain untouched. The local Generate wrapper's post-success report also had references to fields absent from its result object; those accesses were corrected and the wrapper passes `py_compile`.
- Re-ran full Generate with seed `73992950049473338`, saved options, local-unverified version status, and automatic installation disabled. It completed all 594 World Loot map packages and all 38 Healing clones. Retoc output: 713 packages and 714 chunks, `compression_methods: []`, 38 distinct `/Game/CV2Randomizer/Healing/...` clone paths, and `verify=verified`. `Spoiler.txt` contains 30 Golden Seed and 8 Golden Blood Bead assignments.
- Artifacts: `workspace/healing-upgrade-runtime-research/full-generate-runs/generated/20261003T213508.836402Z-73992950049473338-fcc0999dde-bdf1d93f/packaged/` (`CV2Randomizer_P.pak` 347 B, `.ucas` 154,114,982 B, `.utoc` 109,502 B, and `Spoiler.txt` 749,185 B). The files are local outputs only; none were installed and no original game files were modified. The package remains local-unverified, and gameplay validation is still pending.
- Updated `docs/design.md`, `docs/rules.md`, and `docs/plan.md` to describe the clone/redirect/unique-identity strategy and correct output. Validation: .NET Release build passed with 0 warnings/errors; Healing FName fixtures passed (4-byte and 2-byte alignment plus ambiguous-layout rejection); focused World Loot tests passed (26); full unittest suite passed (358 tests, 8 optional local-asset skips); `compileall` and the corrected wrapper's `py_compile` passed.

## Early Blood Code owner correction — 2026-10-04
- Re-audited the exact installed references after gameplay showed Noah_01 and Guardian's Heart still vanilla. Both are owned by `BuddySettings.BloodCode`: Noah by `/Game/Blueprint/Characters/NPC/Buddy/BuddyCharacters/noah/DA_BuddySettings_Noah` and GuardianHeart by `/Game/Blueprint/Characters/NPC/Buddy/BuddyCharacters/GuardianHert/DA_BuddySettings_GuardianHert`. Each primary export has one exact `BloodCodeArticleData` import with FName Numbers `0/0`. The mirrored `BloodCodeArticleDatas[1/2]` references on `QSTask_BloodCodeAddProficiency_0` in `e10210` and `e10250` are proficiency metadata, not the reward owner. `DA_BuddySettings_NoahAnother` has the same Noah_01 import but sits under `/Another/` and has no serialized incoming package references in the extracted source inventory; it remains unpatched.

The prior probe had incorrectly routed it into `INTERACTION_AREA_EVENT`, creating noisy, misleading item rows. The new probe separates `INTERACTABILITY_EVENT`, true `INTERACTION_AREA_EVENT`, generic overlaps, pickup hooks, and interaction actions. It reads only identities for the two object arguments to `ActInteraction` (`InInteractiveActor` target and `InActorWhoInteractWith` actor), with no gameplay property or struct reads.

No `.pak/.ucas/.utoc`, `Mods/mods.txt`, production Randomizer code, game asset, or save was modified. No standalone Lua parser is installed; UE4SS startup will validate the new argument-identity hook on the next run.

## Cross-PC seed determinism and reproducibility fingerprints (2026-10-06)
- Audited every assignment planner and the input/discovery paths. Production planners no longer use Python's `random.Random`, global RNG state, or Python/.NET runtime hashes. Independent feature and intentionally shared pools keep named seed domains; the fixed application-owned `StableRandom` is versioned and uses SHA-256 domain derivation, SplitMix64, rejection-sampled bounds, descending Fisher-Yates shuffle, and partial Fisher-Yates sampling. Fixed algorithm vectors and fresh-process tests protect its behavior.
- Canonicalized order-sensitive inputs using locale-independent NFC/case-fold ordering with an explicit original-spelling tie-break; package identities normalize slash direction. Planner results from filesystem/tree discovery are normalized before use, concurrent inspection results are stored by stable keys/indices, and generated Spoiler lines sort by stable destination identity before presentation labels.
- Reproduced an order-sensitive World Loot selection in the forced candidate-overflow branch: with five canonical candidates and four destination slots, reversing discovery order changed the sampled set (`canonical:test:1` versus `canonical:test:3`). Canonical sorting removes that dependency. The current catalog-only audit counts 184 raw catalog candidates versus 1,788 potential singleton slots, before installed-package availability filtering, so this specific overflow branch is not active in the current catalog. This synthetic reproduction does not prove it caused the other player's reported cross-PC mismatch. No second physical PC/cache was available to reproduce that report directly.
- Added `RANDOMIZER_ALGORITHM_VERSION = 1`. All active and dormant planners now use the fixed PRNG. Existing seed results can differ from builds that used the previous RNG; algorithm v1 is the new reproducibility baseline. UI-only changes do not require a version bump.
- Added SHA-256 `content_fingerprint` over the app version/build identity, every data-root-relative runtime data file (including exclusions, classifications, catalogs, and version definitions), effective source availability, cache identity/revisions, extracted package inventory, and cached package bytes. Added a canonical `config_fingerprint` over generation-affecting options. Install/cache/output paths, timestamps, usernames, and process state are excluded.
- Every Generate now writes canonical `assignment_manifest.json` before patching and reports its SHA-256 in the manifest, `Spoiler.txt`, logs, and Diagnostic. Successful install cleanup retains the assignment manifest beside the spoiler. The manifest records canonical assignments for World/Healing, Shops/NPC Traders, Enemy Drops, Blood Codes, Boosters, Booster Crafting, Initial Inventory, direct Defensive Gadget grants, and Bequeathed Formae.
- Regression coverage: ten repeated plan/manifest/Spoiler runs yield one hash pair; two fresh Python processes with different `PYTHONHASHSEED`s and independent game/cache/output roots, reversed location/catalog/metadata order, and identical logical cache bytes produce the same manifest and Spoiler hashes. Ten representative option states (baseline/all-off, all-on, Shops mix on/off, Enemy Drops, Blood Codes, Boosters, Booster Crafting, Healing, Initial Inventory) rebuild their plans twice and assert exact assignment-manifest hash equality. The existing 256-option planner matrix remains covered.
- Synthetic independent-root hashes: assignment manifest `80d59b389aa69c87137d8a4b783f29c626a35652fbe39fbc3c19b2e53dd48a69`; Spoiler bytes `b5e5aa4ea1a8722f7e066d2796422d8c7c657e9725c8b1352106d5b5a1fbaa57`. These identify the regression fixture only, not a real game/cache pair.
- Validation: full suite `391 passed, 8 skipped, 20 subtests passed`; focused determinism/pipeline/UI/spoiler/core rerun `77 passed`; the 256-option plus repeated-hash configuration test passes; `compileall` passes; audit found no planner/global `random.Random`, Python `hash()`, or .NET `GetHashCode()` use.
- Real two-PC Generate comparison and Retoc validation remain unverified. The only detected game is local-unverified (`local-unverified-524ad82b…`) and its cache state is `NOT_EXTRACTED`; no real output was generated, installed, or verified in Retoc, and no game installation files were changed. The skipped real-asset tests require extracted source packages and `CV2.usmap`.

# Installer access-denied recovery and successful-run log retention (2026-10-07)
- Diagnosed the failed Generate & Install from its retained `generation_failure.txt`: Retoc had already produced and verified all 836 packages (`compression_methods: []`, `verify: verified`), then Windows returned `WinError 5` while replacing the staged `.pak` in the game's `~mods`. The destination folder grants the current user write access; no game process was running during this later inspection, so the original access denial is consistent with a transient lock or another process but does not prove which process held it.
- The installer now retries atomic package replacement three times with short delays. Persistent `PermissionError` reports the exact target and tells the user to close Code Vein 2/other file users or check `~mods` permissions. Existing app-owned files remain under the installer's backup/rollback flow, and unrelated mods are untouched.
- Successful cleanup now removes bulky generated assets from owned run folders while retaining each `logs/` directory and its contents. Spoiler, assignment manifest, READY extraction cache, and run logs remain available; generated staging/package/intermediate folders are removed. The ownership marker keeps log-only workspaces recognizable for later cleanup without deleting their logs.
- Added regressions for a transient install denial, a persistent denial preserving the old trio, and retention of current and prior run logs while heavy workspace content is removed. Validation: focused installer/core/pipeline tests `54 passed`; full suite `396 passed, 8 skipped, 20 subtests passed`; no real install/gameplay was run as part of this source fix.

## Full installed-game physical World Loot pickup audit (2026-10-07)

Added a regression for extracting a parent map after a generated child.

The shared extractor now sends the exact `CodeVein2/Content/...` package path with `.uasset` and `.umap` suffix filters. Retoc dry-runs selected exactly one package for the persistent-map filter and exactly 50 for the DLC-cell batch; the retried 50-map extraction completed with a 0.07 GiB temporary output. The focused extractor tests pass (`16 passed`).
- The sweep has resumed at map index 32,100 with the precise filters; scan progress is being checkpointed in 50-map chunks. The interrupted Retoc run left one audit-owned temporary directory at `workspace/world-ground-pickup-full-audit/cache/821febd7a742b81152ec132199d56939291d34400f5b59af362ce07ae3af4864/.retoc-batch-779ysv_2` (about 42.83 GiB). ## Profile and speed up ready-cache Generate (2026-10-08)
- Profiled Generate against the existing READY cache with the same all-options seed, catalogs, staged package list, and Retoc; all benchmark runs used `install_after_generation=False`. The game's install and `~mods` were not touched. Detailed per-package timings and the 2/3/4-worker runs are retained under `workspace/_generate-performance-current-20261008/`.
- Raised only the World Loot helper scheduler ceiling from two to four. Existing 32-map deterministic batches, 2.5-GiB per-helper estimate, RAM reserve/admission, append-only NameMap checks, raw payload preservation, canonical result ordering, and final structural validation remain unchanged. The global Extract worker ceiling was not changed.
- On the controlled all-options comparison, the 2-worker reference took 399.64s overall and 319.24s for map patching. Two 4-worker runs had a 300.06s median overall and 219.94s median map patch stage: 99.58s (24.9%) faster overall and 99.30s (31.1%) faster in map patching. Median process-tree CPU rose from 678.38s to 741.26s; mean CPU across 16 logical processors rose from 10.61% to 15.44%; peak process-tree RSS rose from 4.13 to 5.36 GiB.
- The default production scheduler then completed all-options Generate in 300.44s, selected four helpers, used 5.37 GiB peak process-tree RSS, built 1,510 packages / 3,020 staged files, and passed `retoc verify`. Installation remained disabled. World Loot grouped 3,722 assignments across 1,284 planned packages; 1,185 changed maps were serialized/reopened in 38 helper batches. Code-path accounting is 3,555 UAssetAPI map loads, 1,185 serializations, and 1,185 final reopen validations; other feature helper loads are excluded from that count.
- Other profiles were each repeated twice at four workers: World Loot only median 214.24s, Shops + World Mix median 214.49s, and Enemy Drops median 222.28s; each passed Retoc verify. The historical one-worker data was not treated as an apples-to-apples baseline. Six/eight-worker requests are clamped to at most four lanes by the existing physical-RAM policy on this 31-GiB machine; that safety policy was not bypassed.
- The largest remaining cost is World Partition parsing/serialization/reopen validation. The slowest changed map took 26.16s; package-level elapsed time not explained by those phases was at most 100ms after rounding. Memory-budget admission accumulated 18.6–29.1s across worker waits in the controlled 4-worker runs, overlapping active processing rather than adding that amount to wall time. File-level disk latency was not directly measured, so no disk or antivirus cause is asserted. A persistent helper was not added because 38 map batches already amortize startup over 1,185 packages, and startup was not isolated as the dominant cost. No binary fast path or cross-system serializer merge was attempted because of World Partition/raw-payload safety.
- Assignment manifest hash, Spoiler hash, package path hash, package count, and container sizes matched between 2- and 4-worker runs. Retoc package paths matched across all 1,510 packages; all 1,185 map results reopened and passed raw preservation. `.ucas` and `.utoc` container hashes differed, so this establishes unchanged assignment-level logical output, not byte-identical containers. Cross-PC determinism and in-game behavior were not tested.
- Updated `docs/design.md` for the four-worker memory-admitted World Loot pool. Added `workspace/generate_performance_detailed.json` and `workspace/generate_performance_before_after.md` with stage wall/CPU, helper and UAssetAPI counts, package timings, 20 slowest packages, 20 longest package residuals, scheduler waits, hashes, Retoc results, and measurement limits.
- Validation: `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest tests/test_world_loot_serialization.py tests/test_memory_budget.py -q` passed (`21 passed, 3 subtests passed`). Report builder `py_compile` passed and both performance artifacts were regenerated. No cache contents, game installation files, or `~mods` were accessed or changed.


## Add guarded World Loot binary fast path (2026-10-08)
- Extract-time World Loot metadata now persists versioned fixed-width ArticleDataPath descriptors containing package/sidecar hashes and sizes, NameMap indices, export/reward identities, exact `.uexp` offsets and expected 16-byte FName bytes. Cache validation rejects malformed descriptors.
- Generate performs a descriptor-only NameMap gate before reading/hashing a map. It only selects direct binary writing when assigned package/object names are already present; full package and sidecar hashes, expected bytes, offset bounds, identity/layout, and non-overlap are then validated. Batched writes preserve file size/header and verify exact changed ranges/readback. Any uncertainty falls back to the existing UAssetAPI serializer. Maps with a Healing per-instance override remain wholly structural.
- Audited the 1,510 changed packages from the same-seed/options, installation-disabled all-options run: 0 `FAST_BINARY_PATCH`, 1,108 `STRUCTURAL_NAMEMAP_REQUIRED`, and 402 `STRUCTURAL_OTHER` (77 mixed World Loot/Healing maps plus 325 packages outside this descriptor path). The 1,108 checked maps required 1,136 unique missing FName literals. The 325 other packages have no per-package timing instrumentation; measured map-package cost sums are recorded separately and overlap across helper workers.
- The binary path made no writes, so it avoided 0 structural map serializations and produced no speedup. The two after runs had identical assignment manifest, Spoiler, and 1,510 package-path hashes, passed all 1,185 structural map reopen checks and `retoc verify`; before/after package-path hashes also match. The old baseline did not retain full assignment rows and used cache preparation revision 6, while the descriptor cache is revision 7, so strict old-to-new same-fingerprint assignment/Spoiler equality is not claimed.
- Median full Generate was 300.06 s before and 341.58 s after; median World Loot map stage was 219.94 s before and 258.51 s after. These measurements show no improvement and do not establish causation; machine scheduling/cache metadata differ. No custom NameMap writer was added.
- Required reports: `workspace/package_patch_strategy_audit.csv`, `workspace/package_patch_strategy_audit.json`, `workspace/binary_patch_benchmark.md`, and `workspace/generate_binary_fastpath_before_after.md`. The 100-package report compares the measured structural path with only the cheap eligibility gate; a binary write benchmark is unavailable because none of the sample packages passed the NameMap gate.
- Validation: focused World Loot binary/generation/dependency/cache tests pass (`34 passed, 2 subtests passed`); `compileall -q src tests tools` passed. No game installation archives or `~mods` were accessed or modified.

## Tune Extract worker ceiling after single-pass integration (2026-10-09)
- Ran a bounded Release-helper sweep on 96 evenly spaced World Loot map packages with 2,606 canonical NameMap values (360 reward rows). All three settings serialized all 96 maps with zero fallback. Two workers took 27.940s; four took 21.655s (22.5% faster); requesting six was physically capped at four and took 22.632s. The 4-worker sample's peak sampled process-tree RSS was 2.711 GiB and minimum system-available RAM was 14.721 GiB. The 6-request run remained bounded at 4 workers and minimum available RAM 14.380 GiB. The scheduler still reserves the greater of 2 GiB or 15% physical memory and admits jobs based on available RAM and observed size.
- Raised `MAX_HEAVY_WORKERS` from 3 to 4. The physical and available-memory caps still limit 8/16/24 GB classes to at most 1/2/3 lanes, and may reduce all of them further. Updated the scheduler test for nominal 8/16/24/32 GB bands.
- Re-ran a full installed-game Extract with the 4-worker setting. The revision-14 cache was promoted to READY successfully in 670.532s (11m10.532s), compared with 721.894s for the earlier 3-worker single-pass Extract and 816.737s for the revision-13 baseline. The final Extract saved 51.362s (7.10%) versus the previous single-pass run and 146.205s (17.90%) versus revision 13. The combined map stage fell from 329.345s to 283.542s. Maps remained 1,454 total: 1,250 prepared/serialized and 204 read-only; source preparation reloads remained zero. Peak child working set increased modestly from 4.772 to 4.935 GiB compared with the preceding 3-worker pass; minimum available RAM remained 11.523 GiB. The atomic Extract finished ready; no low-memory fallback or cache loss occurred.
- Two same-seed, all-options Generates ran from the final 4-worker Extract cache with installation disabled. They took 117.635s and 119.086s; both produced 1,504 package paths and 3,008 staged files. Manifest, Spoiler, content fingerprint, package paths, and staged-file SHA-256 values match. Retoc `info` reports `compression_methods: []`, `list --path` reports 1,504 packages, and `verify` passes. World Loot binary patching remained 1,112/1,112 with zero structural fallback; Healing used 110/112 prepared map overrides and 110 clones, with two unrelated Treasure Box fallbacks. All 2,644 source-occurrence IDs remain unique. Temporary Generate output directories were removed after the checks.
- Updated the final Extract profile and per-map CSV. The largest exclusive stage is now static dependency/prepared-cache generation (321.910s), followed by single-pass map discovery/preparation (283.542s) and required/optional source extraction (278.960s). The full Extract profile has no comparable CPU or disk-throughput baseline; sweep CPU and disk counters are system-wide and explicitly labeled as such.
- Reports and rerunnable bounded benchmarks: `workspace/extract_worker_sweep.md`, `workspace/extract_worker_sweep.json`, `workspace/extract_worker_sweep_generate_regression.md`, `workspace/extract_worker_sweep_generate_regression.json`, and `workspace/update_extract_single_pass_profile.py`. Updated the required single-pass profile, before/after report, slowest-package CSV, and design notes.
- No game archives or `Paks/~mods` contents were accessed or modified. Generate installation remained disabled.

## Persist installed package inventory for repeat Extracts (2026-10-09)
- Added a compact gzip package inventory index under the extracted-cache root. The cache key binds to the installed archive fingerprint, current `.utoc` container names, index schema revision, and Retoc version. A corrupt or stale index safely misses and rebuilds through Retoc; `Clear Extracted Game Data` removes it with the rest of the extracted cache. The inventory remains read-only metadata and does not replace READY asset validation.
- Added regressions proving index reuse performs zero Retoc `list` launches, while a changed `.utoc` or Retoc version forces a fresh listing. Against the installed game, a cold five-container listing found 216,218 paths in 3.063s; a new extractor reused the same inventory in 0.990s with no `list` launches. The smoke test used a temporary cache and removed it afterward.
- The source extraction/cache portion of the already completed cold Extract improved by 42.102s (15.1%). The full Extract took 686.927s versus the 670.532s baseline because map preparation took 54.476s longer; therefore this run does not establish a total Extract speedup. The inventory-index smoke timing is separate and was not included in that Extract measurement.
- Validation: focused regression suites passed (`107 passed, 6 subtests`), `compileall` passed, and the real-install package-index smoke passed with matching cold/warm inventories. Two same-seed all-options Generates remain verified with installation disabled. `~mods` and installed game archives were not modified.
- Updated reports: `workspace/extract_dependency_cache_profile.json`, `workspace/extract_dependency_cache_profile.md`, `workspace/extract_dependency_cache_before_after.md`, and `workspace/extract_dependency_duplicates.csv`; real-game inventory smoke: `workspace/extract_package_inventory_index_smoke.json`.

## Clarify low-memory Extract failures (2026-10-10)
- Investigated the reported World Loot NameMap preparation failure. At the failed admission check Windows reported 6,426 MiB free; the job estimate was 2,560 MiB and the 15% physical-memory reserve was 4,774 MiB, leaving a 908 MiB shortfall. The scheduler correctly refused the job and preserved the previous READY cache.
- Improved the error text to distinguish total available memory, memory remaining after the safety reserve, estimated job memory, and shortfall. The reserve and admission behavior are unchanged.
- At follow-up inspection the system had about 15,990 MiB available, enough to retry the same Extract action. Validation: `tests/test_memory_budget.py` passed (`4 passed, 4 subtests`) and `compileall` passed. No game files or `~mods` were changed.
- Follow-up for smaller-RAM PCs: reduced only the scheduler's minimum reserve floor from 2 GiB to 1 GiB; the 15%-of-physical-RAM reserve remains, so it is still about 4.77 GiB on this 32 GiB PC. The available-memory and physical-worker caps continue to restrict an 8 GB-class machine to one heavy worker. A simulated 8 GB profile with 4 GiB available now admits the default 2.5 GiB job while retaining its proportional reserve. This is scheduler-policy coverage, not a claim that a full Extract was run on an 8 GB computer. `tests/test_memory_budget.py`, `tests/test_world_loot_dependencies.py`, and `tests/test_game_data_cache.py` passed (`35 passed, 6 subtests`); `compileall` passed.
- Follow-up to the next user report: capped the proportional reserve at 3 GiB. At 32 GiB physical RAM, the previous 15% reserve demanded about 4.77 GiB plus the 2.5 GiB helper estimate, rejecting the screenshot's 6,814 MiB-available state by 520 MiB. With the cap, one default heavy job is admitted in that exact simulated state while retaining 3 GiB of headroom; smaller machines continue to use the 1 GiB floor / 15% proportional reserve and the existing physical-worker cap. Added regression coverage for the reported memory snapshot. Full Extract on low-memory hardware is still not measured.
- Updated the policy after clarifying the desired low-memory behavior: memory estimates and reserve now limit parallel workers only. The scheduler always admits one heavy job, even below the estimate/reserve threshold, and waits for active work to finish before starting another worker when memory is tight; it no longer times out and blocks Extract/Generate solely due to available RAM. Added regressions for a single worker below the memory estimate and serialization of queued work. A true Windows commit exhaustion can still fail the underlying helper, but package/cache promotion remains transactional. Validation: memory scheduler, World Loot dependency, World Loot serialization, and game-data-cache suites passed (`54 passed, 6 subtests`); `compileall` passed.

## Disable unsafe free Booster extraction option (2026-10-10)
- Blueprint inspection found `GetCreatableCount` divides each material inventory count by that row's `UseNum`; setting `UseNum` to zero creates an invalid recipe calculation. The user's in-game screenshot showed UI corruption and the generic extraction failure after that patch.
- Disabled the option in the UI, ignore any previously saved checked value, and fail closed in generation if a direct request still enables it. Normal recipe reward randomization leaves its costs intact.
- A correct zero-material option requires a safe change to the game's calculation and extraction transaction; the current recipe-only patch cannot provide it. The user should uninstall the broken generated mod before playing, then generate a new one from an updated app build.

## Trace the Booster extraction failure (2026-10-10)
- Audited the extracted `WDT_ItemSyntheticMenu`, `WDT_ItemSynthetic_RecipeMenu`, `WDT_ItemSynthetic_RawMaterialMenu`, `BP_ItemSyntheticUIObject`, and `BP_FieldMenuUIObject` Blueprint bytecode. Booster confirmation calls the native `/Script/Extensions.ItemSyntheticMenuUIObject::BoosterSyntheticRequest`; when it returns false, the menu checks material shortage and Haze shortage before displaying the generic Japanese failure text.
- Confirmed that zero material quantity makes the visible shortage calculation pass, while `UseExp` remains a separate Haze requirement. The remaining failure is inside the native Booster request/transaction, whose implementation is not present in these Blueprint packages. The shared count path also divides by `UseNum`, so zero must be handled there if a complete bypass is attempted. Removing the material array was correlated with the UI corruption reported in-game; the menu has five fixed raw-material widgets.
- Recorded findings in `workspace/free_booster_extraction_audit/findings.md` and updated the design constraint: recipe-row edits alone are insufficient. A safe solution must skip material checks/consumption, retain Haze spending, grant the Booster, and preserve the menu's material widget structure. Free extraction remains disabled pending that implementation. Corrected the UI tooltip and fail-closed error to describe the observed Booster-request failure instead of attributing it solely to the shared craft-count division. No tests were run; no installed game files, generated mod, or `~mods` contents were modified.

## Skip optional content missing from a game build (2026-10-10)
- Clarified cross-build availability: packages catalogued from DLC or other game versions are optional unless explicitly required as core data. Their absence is recorded by Extract and does not invalidate the cache; Generate skips affected content while continuing with available locations.
- Generate now skips a Jail identity if its unique physical source package/location is unavailable, preserving the one-copy rule. Bequeathed Forma direct rewards retain available grant routes individually instead of disabling the entire category when one alternate quest package is absent.
- FieldEnemy destinations are now filtered independently by installed Quest package; one missing destination no longer disables the other available drops. Updated its source manifest/hash and the design notes.
- Added focused cache, Jail, Bequeathed Forma, FieldEnemy, Stinger-route, and availability tests. All focused suites passed (`75 passed, 2 subtests passed`), the full generation pipeline suite passed (`36 passed`), and Python `compileall` passed. No game files, `~mods`, or release artifacts were touched.

## Confirm Booster Extractor material consumption (2026-10-10)
- In-game test with the diagnostic setting `Booster Material Cost Test (1 each)` succeeded. The game consumed one of each listed ingredient and continued to charge the recipe's Haze cost.
- This confirms that the native Booster craft honors positive material quantities and consumes them; the data-table-only patch cannot make them free. Zero quantities and removing material rows remain invalid based on prior tests.
- Found a native grant call in the game's quest Blueprint: `InventoryManager:AddArticleToPlayer(ArticleBulkInfo)`, accessed through `GetWorldSubsystem`. The schema accepts Article paths and counts, so an option could preserve the normal positive-cost craft and refund those same materials only after the native request succeeds, leaving Haze spent.
- The synthetic menu's recipe UI result omits material Article paths; it contains only each ingredient's icon, localized name, cost, and owned counts. The source `ItemSyntheticRecipeInfo` table has the paths, but the current `UIBoosterQueryInfo` category/index has not yet been mapped safely to an exact recipe row. The grant call is a promising route, not yet a complete or tested implementation. Kept the free-material option disabled; no app behavior or `~mods` files were changed.

A refund must use the generated seed's assigned Booster categories and the native menu's verified recipe ordering.

## Fix global ground ingredient type randomization (2026-10-11)
- The previous planner restricted replacements to ingredient classes already referenced in a destination map and grouped types by their direct Blueprint parent. This left many maps unchanged and did not match the requested rule. Ingredients now use one deterministic type permutation across all validated ingredient classes: every pickup of type A becomes type B everywhere, regardless of the map or validated parent family.
- Generate can add a missing replacement Blueprint/CDO import to a staged map, then patches and reopens the map while preserving its opaque actor data and `UniqueSaveID`. It supports audited `RawExport` pickups as well as parsed actors. If any instance of a type is unsafe, that whole type remains vanilla to keep all its pickups consistent.
- The spoiler emits one line per changed ingredient type and reports how many canonical ground pickups use that mapping; mirrored map records are omitted from the line and count.
- Validation: 64 focused material, serialization, spoiler, and pipeline tests passed; UAssetAPI Release build passed with 0 warnings and errors. A cached real map containing an opaque pickup was patched and reopened successfully, with its `.uexp` unchanged. No Extract cache, installed mod, or `~mods` files were changed.

## Normalize renewable material UniqueSaveID format (2026-10-11)
- The Generate error values were the same GUID in two textual layouts. UAssetAPI/.NET prints GUID fields in .NET byte order, while the catalog uses Unreal `FGuid` word order. The helper now reconstructs the four little-endian 32-bit words before formatting and comparing IDs. For example, map text `f9fc2a63-6f00-444b-b1ae-dd254ea3d616` normalizes to catalog text `F9FC2A63-444B-6F00-25DD-AEB116D6A34E`.
- UniqueSaveID validation remains strict for every catalogued pickup, including mirrored maps. No mirror-only bypass is used. Parsed actors are checked before and after patching; if UAssetAPI reopens a patched actor as opaque, package sidecars must remain byte-identical.
- Regression coverage keeps GUID comparison case-insensitive after normalization and rejects the raw .NET field ordering. Updated stale foundation-test counts to the current catalog (2,390 BASE locations and 152 BASE rewards). No installed mod, game files, or `~mods` contents were changed.

## Reopen material actors after class reassignment (2026-10-11)
- UAssetAPI can reopen a patched actor using a different parser representation (`NormalExport` versus `RawExport`) after its Blueprint class reference changes. That parser classification is not itself a package-data change.
- Patched targets may now move between those two supported parser representations. Validation still checks target name, assigned class/CDO references, and unchanged package sidecars; parsed exports also compare their GUID/property snapshot whenever the original representation provided one. Unmodified exports must keep their original parser type and class/template indices.
- Validation: 72 material, pipeline, and foundation tests passed; Python compile checks passed; the UAssetAPI Release helper built with 0 warnings and errors. Both GUID conversions from the reported logs were checked against their catalog IDs. No game, installed mod, or `~mods` files were modified.

## Preserve material actor payload when adding map imports (2026-10-11)
- Generate failed while preparing renewable-material map patches because UAssetAPI rewrote or omitted `.uexp` during a header write that adds class/CDO imports. The helper now restores the original `.uexp` onto the staged package before checking byte identity. Those imports and later class/template reference edits are in the `.umap` header; actor payload remains unchanged.
- Validation: 72 material, pipeline, and foundation tests passed; Python compile checks passed; the UAssetAPI Release helper built with 0 warnings and errors. No game files, generated mod, or `~mods` contents were modified.

## Investigate missing second MagMell Hot Spring reward (2026-10-12)
- The user retested the installed seed and confirmed only Acidic Weapon Salve was granted; no Jadwiga Coin was received. The vanilla MagMell quest task contains two unique recipe grants, Stone Vaccine and Stall Vaccine.

Then extracted the installed `CV2Randomizer_P.utoc` alongside the game’s global ScriptObjects container and parsed the MagMell package inside the mod through UAssetAPI. Export 10 contains both assigned Article paths at reward indices 0 and 1, with count 1, notification enabled, and array length 2.

The observed missing Coin may be a load-priority or game grant discrepancy; do not treat the spoiler or package inspection alone as proof of in-game delivery. Next isolate whether reward index 1 fails generally or whether Jadwiga Coin is a special-case reward.

UAssetAPI reopened the packed asset and confirmed both entries, count 1, notification enabled, and array length 2; Retoc verification passed. The user tested it and again received only Acidic Weapon Salve, with no Throwing Knife. Their same-save vanilla screenshot shows both Stall Vaccine and Stone Vaccine, confirming that the modded MagMell package loses the second runtime reward.
- The user tested the crossover probe and received Tender Light Stone 4 plus an item displayed as **Uncanny Stone**. The patched Article path was `DA_Item_JadwigaCoin_1`, matching the user's suspicion that “Jadwiga Coin” is an internal asset name. The user then clarified that an Uncanny Stone may only produce a new inventory item/notification the first time it is obtained; if already present, later grants may not visibly add another. Therefore, the earlier Acid Salve + Uncanny Stone result (only Acid shown) does not establish that reward index 1 failed, and the crossover only confirms the first visible acquisition.
- A slot-1 retest package was prepared at `workspace/magmell-coin-slot1-retest-diagnostic` with Tender Light Stone 4 in Articles[0] and `DA_Item_JadwigaCoin_1` in Articles[1]. Retoc and UAssetAPI verify the package, but it is not a valid visible-inventory test on the user's current save after acquiring an Uncanny Stone. A future test must observe Jadwiga quest progress or use a save where the reward has not already been acquired. No game installation files were modified.
- At the user's request, built `workspace/magmell-repeatable-materials-diagnostic` with two ordinary weapon upgrade materials already seen in earlier reward tests: `DA_Item_MatLevelUpWeaponA_01` at Articles[0] and `DA_Item_MatLevelUpWeaponB_04` (Tender Light Stone 4) at Articles[1], one of each. This tests repeat stack increments and both reward slots without the Uncanny Stone's possible first-acquisition behavior. Retoc verification and UAssetAPI readback passed; both paths, counts, notifications, and the two-entry array were confirmed. Package is ready but not installed; the user should record On Hand values and ensure neither item is at its cap. No game installation files were modified.
- The user tested that package and reported receiving only Tender Light Stone 4. Built `workspace/magmell-repeatable-materials-reversed-diagnostic` with the same materials swapped; Retoc packaging plus UAssetAPI reopen validation confirmed B04 at Articles[0] and A01 at Articles[1], both count 1 with notifications enabled.
- The user tested the reversed package and again received only Tender Light Stone 4, and clarified that the A01 inventory count did not increase. This confirms A01 is not being added in this test, rather than merely lacking a notification. B04 was delivered from either reward index, so a general second-slot failure is unlikely. The current inventory CSV lists A01 as repeatable (`is_one_time=false`) with 40 ordinary World Loot occurrences; the user's later On Hand check showed A01 at 1/99, ruling out a full carry limit for this result.
- Built `workspace/magmell-repeatable-materials-a02-diagnostic` as a new control: B04 at Articles[0], and common repeatable `DA_Item_MatLevelUpWeaponA_02` at Articles[1]. The CSV lists A02 as `is_one_time=false` with 75 ordinary World Loot occurrences. Retoc packaging plus UAssetAPI reopen validation confirmed both assigned paths, counts, notifications, and reward indices. This checks whether the failure is specific to A01. No game installation files were modified.
- The user tested the A02 control and received both Tender Crimson Stone 2 (A02) and Tender Light Stone 4 (B04); they confirmed neither was previously in their inventory. This verifies MagMell grants two distinct entries when both items are new, but does not by itself establish repeat-grant behavior. No game installation files were modified.
- The user confirmed A01 was at 1/99 and still did not increase after the bath, then reported that the bath only grants an Article when it is not already in inventory. Together with successful first-time grants for A02/B04, this is evidence that the MagMell Hot Spring reward task suppresses duplicate owned Articles instead of increasing their stack. Keep this conclusion scoped to Hot Spring task grants until another reward source is tested. No game installation files were modified.

All 10 extracted Hot Spring reward tasks set this flag to true; among 96 other extracted quest packages with parsed `QSTask_GainItem` exports, none set it true. This is strong evidence for a Hot Spring-specific duplicate-gain rule, though the engine meaning is inferred from the field name and matches the user's inventory tests.

Updated the normal World Loot quest path to clear that flag on all registered Hot Spring tasks, including when an assignment maps to the same Articles; self-mapped Article paths are left untouched. The shared UAssetAPI patcher validates the source flag, changes only this flag plus actually reassigned Article paths, then reopens and checks the output. No game archives were modified.

Parsed all 96 locally extracted Quest packages: 105 `GainItem` task exports, zero parse failures; the flag is explicitly serialized as true on the 10 Hot Spring tasks only. The other 95 task exports omit a serialized value for the flag (likely the default false). ## Use the structural Blueprint identity for renewable ingredients (2026-10-13)
- The failed Generate showed that `WorldLootLocation.actor_class` is the prefix of an actor export's instance name, not necessarily its serialized `ClassIndex`. - The initial variant fix incorrectly treated that instance-name prefix as the actor Blueprint class. Material conversion and catalog parsing now take the structurally catalogued package/name as the actual class identity for both reset-profile validation and the patcher's expected original class. Renewable classes without a validated reset profile still stay locked.
- The patcher error now reports the map, export, and expected/observed class identities if this check fails again.

Full Python suite passed (`571 passed, 9 skipped, 25 subtests`); the helper built without warnings or errors. Rebuilt the standalone Windows app and ZIP in `release/0.5.1-material-class-fix`; both packaged startup/resource smoke tests passed. No mod was generated or installed.
